### C23 – Secure storage, show/hide buttons, sync

Lines 18891–19340 of `knackFunctions.js`.

| Function | Lines | Verdict | Current signature → proposed | Sig Δ | Shim | Risk | Wave | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `initSecureStorage` | 18895–18931 | modernise | `async initSecureStorage(storageKey)` | no | no | medium | 1 | Small async function with a clear async/await gain and triple-nested error handling that violates the error-reporting policy. Only in-file consumers are the two sibling storage functions and setupLocalStorageMinutes (L13282). Medium risk only because the behaviour cannot be exercised without a KTL stub and the flag semantics are easy to get subtly wrong. Changes: L18900-18931: replace the .then/.catch chain inside an async function with straight await: `await ktl.storage.initSecureLs(); const raw = store.get(storageKey); ...` - one try/catch instead of three nested ones.; L18929-18930 re-wrap L18924's already-wrapped error ('Secure storage initialization failed: Failed to get item from secure storage: ...'); throw once with a single message and report via errorHandler.handle(err, {storageKey}, 'initSecureStorage').; L18907 console.log on first initialisation is noise - drop it; L18917 console.warn on corrupt JSON becomes errorHandler.handle(parseError, {storageKey}, 'initSecureStorage') before the reset write (resetting user data must not be silent).; L18903/18909/18919 hard-code the KTL flags (noUserId=false, session=false, secure=true) three times; route through the proposed secureJsonStore(storageKey) primitive built on a generalised createStorageAdapter (L1857-1930 already does the KTL detection and try/catch wrapping).; L18896-18898: `return Promise.reject(new Error(...))` inside an async function is identical to `throw new Error(...)`; use throw.; Guard `globalThis.ktl?.storage?.initSecureLs` and reject with a clear 'KTL not loaded' Error instead of the implicit ReferenceError that surfaces today when KTL is absent. Risks: Error message text changes if the double wrapping (L18924 + L18930) is collapsed; any consumer matching on error.message would notice.; The three boolean flags passed to lsGetItem/lsSetItem (L18903, L18909, L18919) mean per-user, non-session, encrypted storage; using the wrong adapter options silently reads a different key namespace and loses existing data.; Callers at L13282 and L18941 call this without .catch, so any rejection is an unhandled promise rejection today; V2 must not turn that into a synchronous throw.; Returning {} and resetting the store on a JSON parse failure (L18919) destroys the user's saved minutes; keep the reset but report it. |
| `setSecureStorage` | 18940–18949 | modernise | `setSecureStorage(storageKey, recordKey, fieldId, valueToStore, daysUntilExpiry = 5)` | no | no | low | 1 | Ten lines whose only problems are a lost-update race, unhandled rejections and a wrong JSDoc return. Keeping the undefined return respects the policy's preference for unchanged signatures; the awaitable form lives on the shared secureJsonStore primitive. Changes: L18941-18948: implement as `void secureJsonStore(storageKey).update(store => { ... }).catch(err => errorHandler.handle(err, {storageKey, recordKey, fieldId}, 'setSecureStorage'))` so the read-modify-write is one call and errors are reported.; L18942-18943: `Date.now() + daysUntilExpiry * 86_400_000` instead of mutating a Date via setDate.; L18944-18946: `const entry = (store[recordKey] ??= {}); Object.assign(entry, { [fieldId]: valueToStore, expirationDate: expiresAt })`.; Serialise writes per storageKey inside the primitive (promise chain keyed by storageKey): two blur events in quick succession (L13288/L13293) each read the store independently today and the second write clobbers the first field's value.; Fix the @returns JSDoc (currently claims an object is returned). A future V3 can return the Promise; keeping undefined now avoids a return-type signature change. Risks: Expiry is stored inside the record object under the literal key 'expirationDate' next to field ids (L18945), and is refreshed on every write; getSecureStorage depends on that exact key - keep it.; recordKey comes from getRecordID() at L13281 and may be null when no record is in the URL, producing a 'null' key; preserve rather than throw.; Serialising writes changes timing (a later write waits for the earlier one) but not results; a rewrite that keeps parallel read-modify-write keeps the existing lost-update bug.; Any rejection today is an unhandled promise rejection (no .catch); surfacing it via errorHandler is additive but will make previously invisible failures visible in consumer logs. |
| `getSecureStorage` | 18955–18966 | modernise | `getSecureStorage(storageKey, recordKey)` | no | no | low | 1 | Twelve lines, already promise-based, only needs Object.entries/Date.now tidy-ups and to share the storage primitive. The odd '' sentinel is public behaviour and is kept. Changes: L18957: `const now = Date.now()`; L18958-18962: `for (const [id, entry] of Object.entries(store)) if (now > entry?.expirationDate) delete store[id]` (also guards non-object entries that would throw today).; L18963 writes the whole store back on every read even when nothing expired; only write when at least one entry was deleted (same observable state, fewer encrypted writes).; L18964: `return store[recordKey] ?? ''`.; Implement on secureJsonStore(storageKey).update(...) so purge + write are serialised with setSecureStorage writes. Risks: Must keep resolving to '' (not null/undefined) for missing records; L13301 `if (!minutes) return;` tolerates either, but external callers may compare to ''.; Entries without an expirationDate never expire (`now > undefined` is false) - keep that leniency rather than treating missing expiry as expired.; Skipping the write when nothing expired is unobservable to callers but means a corrupt-then-reset store is only persisted by initSecureStorage; make sure the reset path still writes. |
| `checkAllRadioAnswered` | 18972–18990 | modernise | `checkAllRadioAnswered(viewId, fieldToExclude = [])` | no | no | medium | 1 | Nineteen lines of old-style jQuery with duplicated parsing, a latent TypeError and a silent type mismatch on exclusions; a native single-pass rewrite is shorter and safer. Medium risk purely because :visible semantics are subtle. No in-file callers; public API. Changes: L18976 and L18984 both walk the DOM and both parse the parent field id; do one pass: `for (const radio of root.querySelectorAll('input[type=radio]'))` building a Map<name, checked:boolean>, then `return [...groups.values()].every(Boolean)`.; Replace jQuery `:visible` (L18976, L18984) with the proposed isElementVisible(el) (`el.checkVisibility?.() ?? el.getClientRects().length > 0`) so semantics match jQuery's layout-box test.; L18977/L18985: `$(this).closest('[id^="kn-input-field_"]').attr('id').split('_')[1]` throws a TypeError when a radio is not inside a kn-input wrapper; use the proposed fieldIdFromInputWrapper(el) which returns null safely.; Accept exclusions as numbers or 'field_123' strings by normalising both sides (`String(x).replace(/^field_/, '')`) - today a string exclusion silently never matches because of parseInt (L18977).; Use getViewRootElement(viewId) (C01 primitive) instead of the `#${viewId}` template, and INPUT_RADIO_SELECTOR (L5) instead of the jQuery `:radio` pseudo. Risks: jQuery :visible is true when the element has any layout box; a native rewrite using `hidden`/style.display checks would differ for radios hidden via an ancestor's display:none or via CSS classes - use the layout-box test.; Radio inputs that Knack or app CSS hide (e.g. custom-styled radios with opacity 0 but still laid out) count as visible in both versions; do not switch to opacity/visibility checks.; Current code counts checked radios vs distinct group names; the Map-based version is equivalent only if groups with zero checked are still recorded - ensure every visible group is entered in the Map.; Behaviour when a group's radios are split between visible and hidden members (only visible ones are considered) must be preserved. |
| `changeCellTextBasedOnCellContent` | 19000–19012 | modernise | `changeCellTextBasedOnCellContent(viewId, records, fieldIdToMatch, fieldIdToChange, textToMatch, newText, bkgColor = 'success')` | no | no | medium | 1 | Thirteen jQuery lines with a per-record header re-query, an unscoped row selector and a crash path on non-string fields. Seven positional parameters would normally justify an options object, but there are no in-file callers to simplify and the policy prefers unchanged signatures, so keep the positional form and fix the body. Changes: L19002: `record[fieldIdToMatch].includes(textToMatch)` throws when the field is missing or non-string (connection fields are arrays, numbers are numbers); use `String(record[fieldIdToMatch] ?? '').includes(textToMatch)`.; L19003: `$(`tr[id=${record.id}]`)` is document-wide and unquoted; use the proposed getRowById(viewId, record.id) (`#${viewId} tr[id="${CSS.escape(id)}"]`) so a record shown in two grids only changes in the target view.; L19004: resolve the column index once outside the loop (`knackNavigator.getRenderedViewColumnIndex(viewId, fieldIdToChange)` or `th.${fieldIdToChange}` cellIndex) instead of re-querying the header for every record; then `row.cells[index]`.; L19005-19009: `Object.assign(cell.style, { backgroundColor: `var(--${bkgColor})`, fontWeight: '700', color: 'black' })` and `cell.querySelector('.knViewLink__label')?.textContent = newText`; also fall back to the cell's own textContent when the cell is not a link (today non-link cells are styled but never retexted).; Fix the JSDoc (L18992-18999 documents `view` object and omits viewId/bkgColor); use template parameter names that match. Risks: Scoping the row lookup to the view is a behaviour change (bug fix): today every grid on the page showing that record id is recoloured; a consumer may unknowingly rely on that.; Retexting non-link cells would also be new behaviour; if kept strictly compatible, only .knViewLink__label content changes.; Inline `var(--success)` (L19006) depends on the consuming app's CSS custom properties; the library ships none. Also used at L11384 and L18208.; jQuery `td:eq(n)` ignores nothing, but Knack grids with grouping rows or the checkbox column from addCheckboxes shift indices only if the header does not shift too; using cellIndex from the actual th keeps parity.; Must be called from knack-records-render.view_x (records argument); re-running after inline edit is the caller's job - unchanged. |
| `toggleShowHideViewContent` | 19020–19027 | deprecate | `toggleShowHideViewContent(buttonSelector, hiddenSelector, arrowSelector, delay, flexOn = false)  // all three 'Selector' params are jQuery objects, not selector strings` | no | yes | medium | 2 | An eight-line jQuery event binder that duplicates the onclick block in showHideViewGroupContent with a different class vocabulary. V2 should own one native bindShowHide primitive and keep this global as a @deprecated shim. Wave 2 with the rest of the show/hide module per the policy's wave table. Changes: L19021: jQuery namespaced `.off('click.showHide').on('click.showHide')` idempotence becomes a WeakMap<Element, AbortController> in bindShowHide (abort previous binding, bind again).; L19022: `hiddenSelector.slideToggle(delay)` -> shared native slideToggle(el, duration, display) hoisted from L13445-13546.; L19023-19024: `toggleClass('down up')` / `toggleClass('active')` -> `classList.toggle('down'); classList.toggle('up'); classList.toggle('active')` with class names supplied as options so the ktl* vocabulary of the group version can reuse the same primitive.; L19025: `flexOn && hiddenSelector.css('display','flex')` is applied even when hiding; in the primitive pass display:'flex' to slideToggle so it is only applied when revealing. Shim: /** @deprecated Use bindShowHide */ function toggleShowHideViewContent(button, section, arrow, delay, flexOn = false) { bindShowHide({ button: resolveElement(button), section: resolveElement(section), arrow: resolveElement(arrow), duration: delay, display: flexOn ? 'flex' : 'block', classes: { active: 'active', up: 'up', down: 'down' } }); } Risks: jQuery slideToggle animates height, padding and margin with 'swing' easing and restores the element's pre-hide display; the native slideToggle at L13445 uses a cubic-bezier and hard-codes marginTop 13px (L13513, L13542) - the shared primitive must not impose that margin on single-view sections.; jQuery `.off('click.showHide')` only removes jQuery-bound handlers; consumers that bound their own `click.showHide` namespaced handler on the button would have had it removed too - unlikely but note.; Setting display:flex on the hide path today (L19025) is immediately overridden by jQuery's final display:none; the primitive must end hidden with display:none for CSS parity.; Arguments are jQuery objects today; the shim must unwrap them (resolveElement) and tolerate empty jQuery sets (jQuery no-ops on empty sets; native code must early-return). |

Shim: /** @deprecated Use bindShowHide({shrinkLink}) */ function shrinkContent(shrinkLink, section, arrow, delay, button) { bindShrinkLink({ shrinkLink: resolveElement(shrinkLink), section: resolveElement(section), arrow: resolveElement(arrow), button: resolveElement(button), duration: delay, classes: { active: 'active', up: 'up', down: 'down' } }); } Risks: classList.replace('up','down') does nothing if 'up' is missing, whereas removeClass('up').addClass('down') always ends with 'down'; use remove+add to keep parity.; Parameter order differs from toggleShowHideViewContent (button last); the shim must keep this order exactly.; jQuery slideUp ends with display:none and restores height/overflow; the native slideUp at L13548 does the same but with different easing/duration curve - visual difference only. |

'flex' : 'block', classes: { active: 'active', up: 'up', down: 'down' } }).; Keep this global as the public entry point for the single-view flavour; showHideViewGroupContent becomes the entry point for the ktl* flavour, both over the same primitive. If Knack ever renders them non-adjacent, wrapAll still groups them (moving the second); wrapElements must do the same.; No inline display:none is applied (unlike L13402 in the group version); content stays visible until the app's CSS for `.show-hide-section` hides it. Do not add a hidden default.; Falls back to decorating the view's existing `section` for other view types (details, rich text, menu); `viewElement.find('section')` may match several sections and addClass hits all of them - iterate all matches in V2.; Class names `show-hide-section` and `box-with-border` plus the showHideId class are the CSS hook and the selector used by showHideViewContent (L19051); must remain. |
| `toggleElements` | 19119–19128 | dedupe | `toggleElements(selectors, show)` | no | yes | medium | 1 | Ten lines that are a jQuery variant of toggleVisibility with a per-index boolean array. Policy names this cluster explicitly; toggleVisibility is already native and becomes canonical, with this global kept as a 5-line @deprecated shim. Medium risk because jQuery's display-restoring show() is not what toggleVisibility does today. Changes: L19121-19126: collapse both branches into one forEach over a normalised array (the per-index boolean array is the only feature toggleVisibility lacks, and the shim supplies it).; Requires toggleVisibility/resolveElements (C01) to accept jQuery objects as input, since consumers may pass `$(...)` collections here (JSDoc L19116 says jQuery is allowed).; Non-boolean `show` values: jQuery toggles when `typeof show !== 'boolean'` (undefined, 1, 'yes'), toggleVisibility toggles only for undefined and coerces anything else with !!; the shim should map `typeof show !== 'boolean' ? undefined : show` to keep jQuery semantics. Shim: /** @deprecated Use toggleVisibility */ function toggleElements(selectors, show) { const list = Array.isArray(selectors) ? selectors : [selectors]; list.forEach((sel, i) => toggleVisibility(sel, Array.isArray(show) ? show[i] : show)); } Risks: jQuery .toggle()/.show() restores an explicit display (e.g. 'block', 'inline', or the pre-hide value) and treats elements hidden by stylesheet rules as hidden; toggleVisibility sets display='' and decides 'hidden' from inline style only (L7120), so an element hidden by a CSS class will not be shown and a CSS-hidden element will be considered visible. Canonical toggleVisibility should switch to a layout-box check (isElementVisible) and set an explicit display when the computed display is still none.; jQuery .toggle(true) on an element with display:none from a stylesheet forces display:block; native display='' leaves it hidden - the most likely silent regression for consumers.; Array inputs may contain jQuery objects, Elements, NodeLists or selector strings mixed; resolveElements must flatten all of them. |

Risks: Name is misleading (returns the record id held by the form, not a form element id); do not 'fix' by returning the form's id attribute.; Knack may not render `input[name="id"]` on add forms; undefined in both versions.; If the hidden input is outside .kn-submit in some Knack layouts the selector misses it in both versions - keep selector identical. |

Risks: `data-notes-sync-bound` (L19158-19160) is a DOM side effect and part of the signature per policy; keep the exact attribute name even though 'notes' leaks the originating app feature.; Guard requires BOTH inputs to be marked (L19158) so re-binding A to a new C after a partial Knack re-render is allowed by design; keep the && not \|\|.; Native Event dispatch does reach jQuery/Backbone delegated handlers (jQuery uses addEventListener), so Knack picks up the change; do not switch to jQuery .trigger which would NOT be seen by native listeners.; Stale listeners on re-rendered elements write to detached inputs harmlessly; a WeakMap-based rebind could remove them but changes nothing observable. |

### C24 – Capitalise, dropdown, assets, file carousel

Lines 19341–20225 of `knackFunctions.js`.

| Function | Lines | Verdict | Current signature → proposed | Sig Δ | Shim | Risk | Wave | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `capitaliseInput` | 19345–19434 | split | `capitaliseInput(viewId, inputSelector, options = { mode: 'title', trim: true, smartWords: true, preserveCapitalAfterPrefixes: [] })` | no | no | low | 1 | Modern code, but 90 lines mixing DOM binding with a 40-line pure string algorithm that re-normalises options on every keystroke (L19361-19372). Splitting out toTitleCase gives a testable primitive, removes the per-event work and halves the function, all without touching the public parameter list. Low risk because the only consumers are apps calling it from render handlers and the transform can be verified with table-driven tests. Changes: L19361-19372: the options destructuring and the sort/trim/filter of preserveCapitalAfterPrefixes run inside the event callback, i.e. on every keystroke; hoist them above the addInputEventListener call so they run once per binding.; L19353 minorWords and L19382-19420 (the whole word-level transform) become a pure top-level helper toTitleCase(str, opts) with a unit test; capitaliseInput keeps only the DOM wiring (resolve view, resolve input, bind, compute, restore caret).; L19375-19376: `let isBlur = e && e.type === 'blur'` -> `const isBlur = e?.type === 'blur'`; L19386: name the guard `const isTypingSpace = e?.inputType === 'insertText' && e.data === ' '` instead of the triple-negative condition.; L19388-19392: the two nested `.replace(..., function (match) {...})` callbacks become arrow functions; lower-case the prefixes once at normalisation (L19367-19370) so L19399 does not call toLowerCase per word per prefix.; L19394-19410: replace the `.some` that mutates `capitalised` from inside the predicate with `const prefix = normalisedPrefixes.find(p => capitalised.length > p.length && capitalised.slice(0, p.length).toLowerCase() === p)` followed by one assignment.; L19379-19421: the mode dispatch becomes an early-return chain (`if (mode === 'all') return str.toUpperCase();` etc.) inside a small `transform(str, e)` closure; drop the `let result = str` accumulator.; L19345 default parameter: the object literal is redundant because L19361-19366 re-defaults every key, but keep the literal verbatim - simplifying it to `options = {}` would trip the verbatim parameter-text check in tests/v2/v2-structure.test.cjs unless an entry is added to signature-exceptions.json.; No JSDoc @example; add one showing the knack-view-render binding so consumers rebind on every render. Risks: The regexes use ASCII `\b` and `\w` (L19387, L19389, L19391), so accented characters (Jose with acute, O'Neil with non-ASCII apostrophes) are treated as word boundaries; a Unicode-aware rewrite (`\p{L}` with the u flag) would change output for existing data - decide explicitly and keep ASCII semantics unless the apps want the change.; smartWords last-word test `idx + word.length !== full.length` (L19417) is evaluated against the untrimmed value during 'input' events, so a trailing space makes the final minor word lower-case until blur trims it; preserving this quirk exactly is safer than 'fixing' it silently.; Caret restoration (L19424-19430) reads selectionStart before assignment and only restores when the input is document.activeElement; inputs of type email/number return null selectionStart and setSelectionRange throws on them in Chromium - the existing `typeof pos === 'number'` guard must survive the rewrite.; Null options: the default parameter only applies for undefined; `options \|\| {}` at L19366 is what protects against an explicit null - keep both.; Only 'input' and 'blur' are bound; no 'change' event is dispatched after rewriting value, so Knack's Backbone form model sees the final value only on the native change/blur - unchanged, but do not add a synthetic 'input' dispatch or the listener will recurse.; DOM references die on knack-view-render; the function must be called from the view's render handler (and getViewRootElement at L19346 supports the #connection-form-view modal case - keep using it rather than document.getElementById).; addInputEventListener (L7963) wires plain text inputs natively (addNative, L8013-8015) and only reaches for jQuery on Chosen selects, jQuery UI datepickers and timepickers (L8017-8110) - exactly the three exceptions the policy allows - so capitaliseInput stays jQuery-free in V2 as long as that primitive keeps the same dispatch. |
| `capitaliseString` | 19442–19452 | keep | `capitaliseString(str, options = { allCaps: true })` | no | no | low | 1 | Already modern and 11 lines; only a null-options guard and a ternary are worth doing. Kept distinct from toTitleCase because its output differs and consuming apps may depend on it. Changes: L19444: `const { allCaps = true } = options;` throws TypeError when a caller passes null explicitly; use `options ?? {}`.; L19446-19451: collapse the if/else to `const trimmed = str.trim(); return allCaps ? trimmed.toUpperCase() : trimmed.replace(/\b\w/g, c => c.toUpperCase());` and drop the inline comment.; Keep the parameter text verbatim for the V2 structure test. Risks: ASCII-only `\b\w` (L19450): the same non-ASCII caveat as capitaliseInput; do not swap in toTitleCase without accepting that 'mcDONALD' would become 'Mcdonald' instead of 'McDONALD'.; Returns '' (not the input) for numbers or null; callers may rely on that for template rendering. |
| `waitGetConxIdFromDetailId` | 19462–19515 | modernise | `waitGetConxIdFromDetailId({ viewId, fieldId, delay = 5000 })` | no | no | medium | 1 | Fifty-four lines that duplicate getConnectionIdFromHtml and carry a real bug (delay vs timeout). Sharing one id-extraction primitive and normalising ids via knackNavigator keeps the signature and return union intact while halving the body. Medium risk purely because of render-timing semantics around the settle delay. Changes: BUG L19466-19470: waitSelector (L8772-8777) reads `timeout`, not `delay`, so the documented 5000ms is ignored and the 10000ms default applies. Pass `timeout: delay`.; L19464: build the selector from `knackNavigator.normalizeViewId(viewId)` and `knackNavigator.normalizeFieldId(fieldId)` so 'view_12'/12 and 'field_5'/5 all work (today 'field_5' yields `.field_field_5` and always resolves null); this matches the tolerance waitGetValueFromDetail gained in PR 85 (CHANGELOG L92-93).; L19476-19512: replace the `new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(..., 100))` wrapper with `await waitMs(100)` (or better, wait directly for `span[data-kn="connection-value"]` inside the detail body so no arbitrary settle delay is needed) and return the computed value directly.; L19478-19497: replace the inline extraction with the proposed shared primitive getConnectionIdsFromElement(element) -> string[]; this function becomes `const ids = [...new Set(elements.flatMap(getConnectionIdsFromElement))]`.; L19512-19514: report through errorHandler.handle(error, { viewId, fieldId }, 'waitGetConxIdFromDetailId') and still resolve null, instead of a bare console.error with a stringified error.; L19470-19474: `returnType: 'elements'` returns a NodeList or an Array depending on the text-condition branch of waitSelector (L8812-8818); `Array.from` at L19478 already normalises it - keep that. Risks: Fixing the timeout bug shortens the real wait from 10s to 5s; slow detail views that currently succeed between 5s and 10s would start resolving null. Consider keeping 10000 as the default value in the parameter list is NOT allowed (parameter text) - instead document the change in CHANGELOG.; Removing the 100ms settle delay (L19477) risks reading the detail body before Knack has injected connection spans; if the selector is changed to wait for the connection span itself, non-connection fields that have no span would wait until timeout instead of returning null fast.; The single-vs-array return union (L19507-19510) is awkward but public; do not normalise to always-array.; Id normalisation changes failures into successes for prefixed field ids - a behaviour change, but strictly additive. |

### C25 – Clipboard, labels, idle watchdog, confirm dialog

Lines 20226–20716 of `knackFunctions.js`.

| Function | Lines | Verdict | Current signature → proposed | Sig Δ | Shim | Risk | Wave | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `copyViewBodyToClipboard` | 20231–20311 | modernise | `copyViewBodyToClipboard(view, keywords)` | no | no | medium | 1 | The function is already native-DOM and short, but it is built on `document.execCommand('copy')`, which is deprecated in every evergreen browser, and on four `ktl.*` calls of which only `hasRoleAccess` is genuinely KTL-specific. The rewrite keeps the `(view, keywords)` signature, the `knackCopyBodyButton` class and the container mutations, swaps in the async Clipboard API with the old path as fallback, and shares a `writeClipboard` primitive with copyTextToClipboard (L11361). Risk is medium only because the clipboard HTML payload must be wrapped so spreadsheet paste keeps working - a thing no unit test currently covers. Changes: L20294-20304: replace the deprecated `document.execCommand('copy')` with `navigator.clipboard.write([new ClipboardItem({ 'text/html': new Blob([html], {type:'text/html'}), 'text/plain': new Blob([content.innerText], {type:'text/plain'}) })])` inside the click handler (user gesture, secure context - Knack is HTTPS), keeping the selection + execCommand path as the fallback for browsers without ClipboardItem. The html payload for table/search views must be wrapped as `<table>${content.outerHTML}</table>` (content is a `tbody`) so Excel/Word paste still recognises rows; the current selection-copy gets that context from the browser serialiser for free.; L20293 and L20303: drop `ktl.core.selectElementContents(content)` / `selectElementContents()` in favour of native `const range = document.createRange(); range.selectNodeContents(content); const sel = getSelection(); sel.removeAllRanges(); sel.addRange(range);` and `getSelection().removeAllRanges()` (only needed on the fallback path).; L20236: `ktl.views.getViewType(viewId)` -> shared `getKnackViewType(viewId)` (= `Knack.views[viewId]?.model?.view?.type ?? ''`, the lookup already used inline at L16129) so the helper works when KTL is not loaded; keep `ktl.core.hasRoleAccess` (L20254) because keyword role options are a KTL concept, but guard it as `globalThis.ktl?.core?.hasRoleAccess?.(keywordOptions) === false`.; L20237-20242 and L20266: collapse the duplicated table/search entries into `const isGrid = viewType === 'table' \|\| viewType === 'search';` and a selectors map `{ table: grid, search: grid, details: ..., list: ... }` built once at module level (it does not depend on viewId until interpolated - or keep a small arrow `bodySelector(viewId, viewType)`).; L20245: `console.log('copyViewBodyToClipboard error. Unsupported view type:', viewId)` -> `errorHandler.handle(new Error(`Unsupported view type ${viewType}`), { viewId, viewType }, 'copyViewBodyToClipboard')` or a plain early return; a console.log is neither an error report nor silent.; L20260-20263, L20270, L20282: move the three inline button styles into one `ensureStyle('knack-copy-body-style', `.knackCopyBodyButton{margin-bottom:10px;width:fit-content}.table-keyword-search>.knackCopyBodyButton{margin-left:10%}...`)` call (C01 primitive). Keep the two container inline styles at L20269 and L20281 exactly as they are - they override Knack layout and a stylesheet rule of lower specificity would not.; L20296-20299: build the feedback message with `capitaliseString` or a template literal and pass `{ ok: successful }` through one `ktl.core.timedPopup(message, ok ? 'success' : 'error', ok ? 1000 : 2000)` call; the try/catch/finally collapses to an `await writeClipboard(...)` with a single catch.; L20286: `function () {` click listener -> arrow; add `{ once: false }` nothing else - keep re-querying `contentSelector` inside the handler (L20287) because Knack replaces the tbody on every records render.; Add JSDoc `@returns {void}` is already present; add a one-line note that the function must be called on every `knack-view-render.view_x` / `knack-records-render.view_x` because the button lives inside the re-rendered view. Risks: Clipboard payload format: today the selection-based copy writes whatever the browser serialises for the selected tbody/section/columns (both text/html with ancestor context and text/plain). A Clipboard API rewrite must emit the same two MIME types and wrap tbody HTML in `<table>` or Excel paste silently degrades to plain text. Firefox supports ClipboardItem write only from 127 (2024) - keep the execCommand fallback.; navigator.clipboard.write is async and rejects when the document is not focused or the permission is denied; the current synchronous `successful` boolean becomes a resolved/rejected promise - map rejection to the existing `Unable to copy` popup (L20289, L20301) so the user-visible messages do not change.; The `.table-keyword-search` container is forced to `display:inline-flex` (L20269) and `.kn-records-nav` etc. to `flex-direction:column` (L20281) on every render; apps may have CSS that depends on these exact inline overrides. Do not move them to a stylesheet.; Button placement order: `appendChild` into the keyword-search box (L20271) versus `prepend` into the nav/list/details container (L20283). Preserve both because app CSS (`.knackCopyBodyButton` margin-left 10% vs 8px) assumes them.; `ktl.core.hasRoleAccess` short-circuits only on the first keyword occurrence (`keywords[keywordName][0]?.options`, L20253); a view with two `_copybody` keywords uses the first. Keep.; If KTL is absent the function currently throws ReferenceError at L20236; guarding `ktl?.` turns that into a silent return. Acceptable (the feature is KTL-keyword driven) but note it. |
| `if (typeof globalThis !== 'undefined') {` | 6893–6900 | remove-dead | `if (typeof globalThis !== 'undefined') { globalThis.copyViewBodyToClipboard = copyViewBodyToClipboard; }` | no | yes | low | 1 | In a classic script the function declaration is the global, so this block cannot change observable behaviour; it is one of six near-identical export blocks in four spellings that V2 should drop wholesale. Only if V2 is ever wrapped in an IIFE/module should a single `globalThis.copyViewBodyToClipboard = copyViewBodyToClipboard;` line go in the compatibility-shims section. Risks: None for consumers: `globalThis.copyViewBodyToClipboard`, `window.copyViewBodyToClipboard` and the bare identifier continue to resolve to the same function via the declaration.; generate-globals.mjs also collects explicit `globalThis.x =` assignments (L51-61); removing the block does not remove the name from eslint/knack-functions-globals.json because the declaration supplies it (L120 of that file). |

Signature, return and DOM effects are unchanged. Changes: L20349-20365: replace the three `let`s and the two-branch if/else with destructuring: `const [labelParts = [], typeParts] = params; const labelTxt = labelParts.join(', '); const selector = typeParts && !typeParts.includes(viewType) ? null : selectors[viewType];` - `params.map(param => param)` at L20359 is an identity map and is deleted.; L20361 BUG: `type[0].includes(viewType[0])` compares the first character of the view type against the whole type string, so a type specifier of 'details' also matches 'list' (contains 'l'), 'table' and 'search' (contain 't'/'s'), and 'search' matches 'table' only by accident of letters. A broader selector would be a visible change - keep, but document.; `innerHTML` write (L20384) is required for the `{strong}`/`{br}` feature; the label text comes from the builder-authored keyword, not from record data, so no escaping is added. Do not switch to textContent.; Knack re-renders the view (and resets the label) on every render/filter/sort/page; the function must keep being called from the field-keyword hook on each `knack-view-render` - it is correctly stateless.; Omitting the options argument throws TypeError today; adding `= {}` / `params = []` defaults would make it a no-op instead. | `idleWatchDogTimeout` | 20391–20505 | split | `idleWatchDogTimeout()` | no | no | medium | 1 | Half of this 115-line, JSDoc-less function is dialog scaffolding that showConfirmationDialog already implements, and the other half mixes logout, fallback-reload and timer bookkeeping into closures that leak across invocations (L20401-20402 remove the DOM but not the first timer). Splitting into the shared `createModalDialog` primitive, a reusable `performKnackLogout`, and a 35-line orchestrator keeps the exact `idleWatchDogTimeout()` global, every id/class the apps style, and the 60 s / 2 s timings, while fixing the double-invocation bug and the invisible-dialog-without-app-CSS problem via zero-specificity defaults. The 115-line body becomes ~35.; L20455-20480: extract `performKnackLogout()` (idempotent via a module-level flag, `Knack.user.destroy()` in try/catch reporting through `errorHandler.handle(err, {}, 'performKnackLogout')`, then the 2 s `.kn-login` check with retry/reload). The inner `try { window.location.reload(); } catch (e) {}` at L20463 is dead - reload() does not throw - and the comment 'navigate to origin' at L20462 describes code that is not there.; L20401-20402 BUG: a second invocation removes the first dialog's elements but not its `autoLogoutTimeout` (L20504) or its resize listener (L20495); the first closure's performLogout still fires after 60 s even if the user pressed 'Stay Logged In' on the second dialog. Using `:where()` keeps specificity at zero so existing app CSS on `#ktl-idle-dialog` still wins; this also lets V2 supply default `position:fixed; inset:0; margin:auto; height:fit-content; z-index:2011; background:#fff; padding:24px` so the dialog is visible even in an app that never styled it (today it renders in normal flow under the overlay).; L20486: `try { ktl.scenes.resetIdleWatchdog(); } catch (e) { /* ignore */ }` swallows silently - `globalThis.ktl?.scenes?.resetIdleWatchdog?.()` expresses the optional dependency without hiding real errors.; L20497-20504: `Math.max(0, autoLogoutMinutes) * 60 * 1000` on a literal 1 -> `const IDLE_CONFIRM_TIMEOUT_MS = 60_000;` with the two explanatory comments folded into JSDoc (the function has none today).; L20407-20417: numeric `top: 0, left: 0` rely on CSSOM coercion; write `'0'` or use `inset: '0'` as showConfirmationDialog already does (L20571). | `if (typeof globalThis.showConfirmationDialog === 'undefined') {` | 20713–20715 | remove-dead | `if (typeof globalThis.showConfirmationDialog === 'undefined') { globalThis.showConfirmationDialog = showConfirmationDialog; }` | no | yes | low | 1 | Function hoisting makes the `typeof === 'undefined'` test false before the statement runs, so this is provably dead code rather than merely redundant. Changes: L20713: in a classic script, FunctionDeclaration bindings are created on the global object during script instantiation (before the first statement runs), so `typeof globalThis.showConfirmationDialog` is 'function' here and the body is unreachable.; The pattern only makes sense in a module or IIFE; V2 is a classic script per policy, so it is deleted. Shim: Nothing to emit: the V2 `function showConfirmationDialog` declaration keeps the global defined and eslint/generate-globals.mjs harvests it from the declaration (L25-27). If V2 were ever wrapped in an IIFE/module, a single `globalThis.showConfirmationDialog = showConfirmationDialog;` belongs in the compatibility-shims section. Risks: None: the block never executed, so removing it changes nothing observable; `globalThis.showConfirmationDialog` stays defined by the declaration and remains listed at L309 of eslint/knack-functions-globals.json. |

### C26 – Keyword handlers and quick toggles

Lines 20717–21765 of `knackFunctions.js`.

| Function | Lines | Verdict | Current signature → proposed | Sig Δ | Shim | Risk | Wave | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `setViewMaxWidth` | 20717–20742 | modernise | `setViewMaxWidth({ key: viewId })` | no | no | low | 1 | Twenty-six-line handler whose only jQuery use is one `.css()` call; it contains an unreachable else branch (L20732-20737) and an unguarded `params[0][0]` that throws on a bare keyword. Rewriting to native style setting with a single regex test halves the body without changing the signature or the inline-style side effect. Changes: L20719/L20740: replace `$(`#${viewId}`).css('max-width', maxWidth)` with `document.getElementById(viewId)?.style.setProperty('max-width', maxWidth)`; bail out early when the element is missing instead of calling .css on an empty collection.; L20725: `params[0][0]` throws TypeError when the keyword has no parameter group (e.g. a bare `_vmxw`); read `const width = String(params?.[0]?.[0] ?? '').trim()` and return with a console/errorHandler message when empty.; L20729-20738: the inner `if (!isNaN(parsedWidth))` at L20732 is always true because the outer condition already returned for NaN, so the else branch at L20734-20737 is unreachable; collapse to `const maxWidth = /^\d+(\.\d+)?$/.test(width) ? `${width}px` : width;`.; L20729: `width.includes('%') \|\| width.includes('px')` does not cover em/rem/vw/calc(); treating any non-pure-number as a literal CSS length (above) covers them without listing units.; L20720-20723: iterate keyword instances via the proposed shared `forEachKeywordInstance(viewId, '_vmxw', ...)` which applies `ktl.core.hasRoleAccess` once, like hideElementsByValue and buttonToUrl do.; Add JSDoc (none today) documenting `_vmxw=600` and `_vmxw=80%` forms.; Note the signature is inconsistent with sibling handlers: it takes only the view and does not accept `keywords`, relying on `ktl.core.getKeywordsByType` returning [] (keep the signature; document it). Risks: jQuery `.css('max-width', '600px')` and `el.style.maxWidth = '600px'` are equivalent, but `.css()` silently ignores invalid values whereas setProperty also ignores them; neither throws, so parity holds.; If several `_vmxw` instances exist the last one wins today (L20722 loop overwrites); keep loop order.; An invalid numeric-looking value such as '12abc' is currently passed through verbatim (parseFloat gives 12, string includes neither % nor px, so maxWidth = '12abc'); keep passing through rather than inventing validation. |
| `hideEmptyFields` | 20744–20751 | modernise | `hideEmptyFields(view)` | no | no | low | 1 | Eight lines of old-style jQuery (`$.trim`, `.each(function)`, `removeElement`) with a direct one-to-one native equivalent and no signature impact. The name/behaviour mismatch (hide vs remove) is worth documenting but not changing. Changes: L20746-20750: replace `$(...).each(function () { if ($.trim($(this).text()) === '') removeElement(this); })` with `document.querySelectorAll(`#${view.key} .kn-detail-body`).forEach((body) => { if (!body.textContent.trim()) body.remove(); })`; `$.trim` is deprecated since jQuery 3.5.; L20748: drop the `removeElement` indirection (L9618, a jQuery wrapper that V2 implementation code must not call) in favour of `Element.remove()`.; Destructure `{ key: viewId }` in the parameter like its siblings and guard `if (!viewId) return;` so a missing view does not query `#undefined .kn-detail-body`.; Move the L20745 inline comment ('used when label still needed but don't want extra space...') into JSDoc; note the function REMOVES the body (not display:none) despite its name. Risks: Only the `.kn-detail-body` is removed; the sibling `.kn-detail-label` stays (that is the stated intent at L20745). A rewrite must not remove the whole `.kn-detail` wrapper.; jQuery `.text()` on one element equals `textContent`, so bodies that contain only images/icons/empty links are removed today and must still be removed (textContent is empty for them).; Removal is permanent for the render; Knack re-creates the details view on the next render event so consumers re-run it. Do not switch to `hidden`/display:none without a policy decision because CSS in apps may rely on the element being absent (e.g. `:empty` or sibling selectors). |
| `hideElementsByValue` | 20754–20780 | modernise | `hideElementsByValue(view, keywords)` | no | no | medium | 1 | Straightforward jQuery removal (three calls), but the body hides two latent defects: an unscoped selector that concatenates text across views and a `borderRadiusLastVisible()` call that can never do anything. Both need an explicit decision, so the risk is medium even though the code is short. Changes: L20768: `$(`.${fieldId} .kn-detail-body`).text()` is not scoped to the view; when the same field appears in two rendered views jQuery concatenates both texts and the comparison silently fails. Scope to `#${viewId} .${fieldId} .kn-detail-body` (flagged as a behaviour decision below).; L20768: `$.trim(...)` (deprecated) -> `element?.textContent.trim() ?? ''`.; L20775: `$(elesToHide.join()).hide()` -> `document.querySelectorAll(elesToHide.join(',')).forEach((el) => { el.style.display = 'none'; })`; make the implicit `join()` separator explicit.; L20776: `borderRadiusLastVisible()` is called with no arguments but its signature is `(viewId, delay)` (L18402) and it returns immediately when `document.getElementById(undefined)` is null, so the call is a no-op today. ### C27 – KnackError

Lines 21766–22918 of `knackFunctions.js`.

| Function | Lines | Verdict | Current signature → proposed | Sig Δ | Shim | Risk | Wave | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `KnackError` | 21772–22918 | simplify | `class KnackError { constructor(options = {}) /* options: sceneId='', viewId='', fieldMap={}, captureUserData=true, captureSystemInfo=true, consoleLog=true, groupSimilarErrors=true, maxErrorsPerMinute=10, ignoredErrors=[], captureKnackContext=true, separateContextFields=true */; ignoreError(pattern); wrapAllMethods(obj, source, additionalInfo = {}); safeExecute(fn, defaultValue, source, additionalInfo = {}); executeFunction(fn, options = {} /* returnDefaultOnError=false, defaultValue=null, captureArgs=true, captureContext=true, rethrowError=true, source=fn.name||'Execute Function', immediate=false, immediateArgs=[], additionalInfo={} */); wrapKnackListener(viewId, eventType, handler, additionalInfo = {}); getCurrentContext() } + underscore methods _setupContextTracking(), _getCurrentSceneId(), _getCurrentViewId(), _getActiveViewId(), _getFirstViewId(), _extractSceneIdFromUrl(), _validateOptions(), _processErrorGroup(errorData), _shouldIgnoreError(error), _isRateLimited(), _generateErrorHash(errorData), _updatePerformanceMetrics(errorData), _captureErrorData(error, additionalInfo, errorSource), _captureUserData(), _captureSystemInfo(), _captureContextData(additionalInfo /* unused */), _formatKnackContext(context), _logToKnackTable(errorData), _getBrowserInfo(userAgent), _getDeviceType(userAgent), _getOperatingSystem(userAgent) + public instance properties options, api (KnackAPI), boundHandleError, performanceData, _errorCount, _lastResetTime, _errorHashes (Map), _lastKnownContext` | no | no | medium | 3 | KnackError is a 2026-era class (v1.3.0, JSDoc throughout, async/await, optional chaining) that the policy places in wave 3: port as-is with dead-code removal and consistency fixes, no signature changes. The rewrite earns roughly 450 lines by collapsing six wrapper variants and three argument serialisers onto one private core, deleting dead branches (never-populated `record`, duplicated context list, duplicated recordId assignment, dead `caAPI` clause, identical `eventSelector` branches) and table-driving the user-agent sniffing, while every public and underscore method keeps its name, parameters and return type. Risk is medium rather than low because this is the error path itself (failures here are invisible), because its two jQuery bindings must survive the jQuery-removal drive, and because several defect fixes (`_getActiveViewId`, Opera detection, en-GB timestamp) alter the text stored in the Knack error table and therefore the grouping hash. The additive `handle()` alias is the one new surface, and it closes a real trap: four call sites in this file use `errorHandler.handle(` while the class only offers `handleError`. Changes: L21789-21800 constructor: replace the eight `x !== undefined ? x : true` ternaries with `options.captureUserData ?? true` etc.; keep `maxErrorsPerMinute` as `Number.isFinite(options.maxErrorsPerMinute) && options.maxErrorsPerMinute > 0 ? options.maxErrorsPerMinute : 10` (the current `\|\| 10` maps 0 to 10 - `?? 10` would not, and 0 would rate-limit every error). Keep `this.api = new KnackAPI({debug:false, showSpinner:false})` eager (L21803): a lazy getter would turn the public `api` property into a getter-only accessor and break `instance.api = customApi`.; L21855 `Object.assign(additionalInfo, this._captureContextData())`: delete it. It mutates the caller's object and is redundant - _captureErrorData recomputes the same context at L22618 and _logToKnackTable deletes exactly those keys from the stored additionalInfo at L22801-22809, so the stored record is byte-identical without it. Work with `const info = { ...additionalInfo }` and call getCurrentContext() once per error instead of twice (each call runs document.querySelector and an activeElement walk).; L21860-21865 grouping: `existingError.record` is always null because nothing ever writes `record` (L22459, L22471). After `const record = await this._logToKnackTable(errorData)` (L21876) do `this._errorHashes.get(hash)?.record = record` so a grouped duplicate resolves the original record as the JSDoc at L21845 promises; return the hash from _processErrorGroup or compute it once in handleError to avoid hashing twice.; L21894-21925 wrapEventHandler: keep `function` (the wrapper must forward the caller's dynamic `this` to the handler) but replace `arguments` (L21912) with rest params `...args` and `handler.apply(this, args)`; `eventType.replace('knack-', '')` -> `eventType.replace(/^knack-/, '')`; the `const context = {...}` literal can be assigned straight to `self._lastKnownContext`.; L21928-21953 setupGlobalErrorHandling: use `this.boundHandleError`-style arrow listeners as now; add an idempotence guard (`if (this.#globalHandlersBound) return this;`) so a second call does not double-log every window error - flagged in behaviourRisks as a (benign) behaviour change; keep the `event.error \|\| event.message` and `event.reason instanceof Error` coercions exactly.; L21960-22247 six wrappers -> one private core `#wrap(fn, {source, captureArgs = true, captureContext = true, additionalInfo = {}, measure = false, onError})` that returns the async wrapper; each public method becomes 3-8 lines that passes its own `onError`: wrapFunction/executeFunction(rethrowError) -> `(e) => { throw e }`; tryCatch -> `(e) => ({success:false, error:e, message:e.message})`; safeExecute/executeFunction(returnDefaultOnError) -> `() => defaultValue`; monitorPerformance/monitorApiCall -> rethrow after adding `responseTime`/`performanceContext`/`apiParams`. Exact per-method catch semantics (rethrow vs default vs result object, `console.error` in wrapKnackListener L22275) are preserved; only bodies shrink.; L21983-21988, L22028-22030, L22208-22213: one `#serialiseArgs(args)` with a single replacer (function -> 'function() { ... }', Node -> nodeName, Window -> 'Window', plus a WeakSet cycle guard so a Backbone view or jQuery object in the arguments cannot throw 'Converting circular structure to JSON' inside the catch block). monitorPerformance's '[Function]' spelling (L22029) is unified to the common one - see behaviourRisks.; L22044, L22433, L22769: JSDoc openers are indented 8 spaces (`        /**`) while the method is at 4 - fix to the repo's 4-space convention.; L22051-22108 monitorApiCall: the positional guess `recordId: args[2]` is wrong for KnackAPI.createRecord(sceneId, viewId, recordData, options) (L14465) - args[2] is the payload. Derive `requestType` first via `[['create','POST'],['update','PUT'],['delete','DELETE'],['get','GET']].find(([k]) => fnName.includes(k))?.[1]`, then set `recordId` only for PUT/DELETE/GET and `requestData` to `args[2]` for POST, `args[3]` for PUT. Replace the if/else-if chain (L22081-L22091) with that lookup; drop `endTime` temporaries (`performance.now() - startTime`).; L22111-22119 getPerformanceMetrics: guard the divide-by-zero when called within the same millisecond as construction/reset: `perMinute: this._errorCount / Math.max(Date.now() - this._lastResetTime, 1) * 60000`; keep the returned shape.; L22138-22151 wrapAllMethods: keep `for...in` (inherited enumerable methods are intentionally wrapped - switching to Object.entries would skip prototype methods on class instances, a behaviour change) but write the body as a single ternary assignment; use `Object.hasOwn` nowhere here on purpose.; L22160-22168 safeExecute: correct the JSDoc to `@returns {Promise<*>}`; do not make it synchronous (that would be a sync->async signature change needing a shim and all existing `await errorHandler.safeExecute(...)` callers already work). Note in JSDoc that `fn` is invoked with no arguments.; L22183-22237 executeFunction: keep the destructured options contract verbatim; replace the trailing `if (returnDefaultOnError) {...} else if (rethrowError) {...}` (L22225-22229) with early returns; the implicit `undefined` when both flags are false is current behaviour and stays.; L22249-22286 wrapKnackListener: both branches of `eventSelector` (L22279-22281) yield `${eventType}.${viewId}` - collapse to one template literal. MUST keep `$(document).on(...)` (L22283): Knack dispatches `knack-*` events with jQuery `.trigger`, which native `addEventListener` cannot observe. Replace `arguments` (L22263) with rest params. Document that only synchronous throws are caught (an async handler's rejection escapes to `unhandledrejection`).; L22293-22314 getCurrentContext: build the object once - `const currentSceneId = this._getCurrentSceneId(), currentViewId = this._getCurrentViewId(); return { ...this._lastKnownContext, currentSceneId, currentViewId, url: location.href, sceneId: this._lastKnownContext.sceneId \|\| currentSceneId, viewId: this._lastKnownContext.viewId \|\| currentViewId };` (keep `\|\|`, not `??`: the current `!context.sceneId` test also replaces '').; L22318-22335 _setupContextTracking: keep the jQuery binding (`knack-${eventType}.any`, L22323) for the same reason as wrapKnackListener; move the event list to `static TRACKED_EVENTS`; optionally add 'record-create' and 'records-render' (documented Knack events that are currently untracked) - flagged as an additive context improvement, not required.; L22340-22357 _getCurrentSceneId: `typeof Knack !== 'undefined' && Knack.router?.current_scene_key` -> early return; DOM fallback should call the shared getBackgroundSceneId() (L18641) and `slice(3)` the 'kn-' prefix instead of `document.querySelector('.kn-scene')` (which can pick a modal scene depending on DOM order) and `.replace('kn-', '')`. `_extractSceneIdFromUrl` (L22406-22414) matches `/scene_(\d+)/` which never occurs in Knack's slug-based hash URLs; keep it as the last resort for compatibility but say so in its JSDoc.; L22374-22393 _getActiveViewId: the id test `startsWith('view_')` (L22382) matches Knack input ids (`#view_x-field_y`), so when a form field has focus the captured contextViewId is e.g. 'view_12-field_3'. Replace the manual walk with `document.activeElement?.closest?.('.kn-view[id^="view_"]')?.id ?? null` (or test `/^view_\d+$/`). L22397 `_getFirstViewId`: `[id^="view_"]` -> `.kn-view[id^="view_"]` for the same reason. Coerce once in _captureErrorData (`errorSource: String(errorSource ?? 'Unknown')`) so `.includes` cannot throw on a non-string source. Replace the running-average block with `(avg * (n - 1) + t) / n` on two lines.; L22584-22627 _captureErrorData: use `static CONTEXT_FIELDS` instead of the inline list (L22596-22600); since context is computed in this method (L22617-22619), drop the copy-from-additionalInfo loop for the six `context*` keys (they are always overwritten by L22618 when captureKnackContext is on, and absent when it is off) but keep copying `knackContext` and `knackContextFormatted` which wrappers pass explicitly (L21921). Keep `errorStack: error instanceof Error ? error.stack : new Error().stack` (capture-site stack for string errors is useful).; L22632-22652 _captureUserData: drop the redundant `this.options.captureUserData` re-check (the caller already tests it at L22608); `Knack && Knack.getUserAttributes` would throw ReferenceError when `Knack` is undeclared (caught by the try, but inconsistent with the `typeof Knack` guard at L22342) -> `const attrs = globalThis.Knack?.getUserAttributes?.(); if (!attrs) return {};` and `globalThis.Knack?.getUserRoleNames?.()`.; L22656-22699 _captureSystemInfo: `_getBrowserInfo(userAgent)` runs twice (L22663-22664) -> destructure once; `Knack ? { name: hit.name, version: ua.match(hit.version)?.[1] ?? 'Unknown' } : { name: 'Unknown', version: 'Unknown' };` - removes the 30-line switch. Ordering bug: 'Opera' (L22834) sits after 'Chrome' and 'Safari' but Opera's UA contains both, so Opera is reported as Chrome; order the table Edge, Opera, Chrome, Firefox, Safari, IE.
