'Unknown OS';` with the table as a frozen static (order matters: Android before Linux, iOS before macOS already correct).; Whole class: add `handle(error, additionalInfo = {}, errorSource = 'Unknown') { return this.handleError(error, additionalInfo, errorSource); }` so `errorHandler = new KnackError(...)` satisfies both call styles used in this file (`.handle(` x4, `.handleError(` x9); bump `@version` to 1.4.0; move `_validateOptions()` (L21824) before `_setupContextTracking()` (L21820) so misconfiguration is reported before listeners are bound; keep all underscore methods public-by-convention (consumers may call `_getCurrentSceneId()` / `_getBrowserInfo()` directly - every property reachable from a global class is public API per the policy).; L21789-21800 constructor: replace the eight `x !== undefined ? x : true` ternaries with `options.captureUserData ?? true` etc.; keep `maxErrorsPerMinute` as `Number.isFinite(options.maxErrorsPerMinute) && options.maxErrorsPerMinute > 0 ? options.maxErrorsPerMinute : 10` (the current `\|\| 10` maps 0 to 10 - `?? 10` would not, and 0 would rate-limit every error). Keep `this.api = new KnackAPI({debug:false, showSpinner:false})` eager (L21803): a lazy getter would turn the public `api` property into a getter-only accessor and break `instance.api = customApi`. COMPAT: `?? true` is not identical to `x !== undefined ? x : true` - an explicit `null` currently disables the option (null is falsy at L21792-21800) but `?? true` would enable it; use `options.captureUserData === undefined ? true : options.captureUserData` or document the null change. The proposed `Number.isFinite(x) && x > 0 ? x : 10` for maxErrorsPerMinute rejects `Infinity` (a working 'no rate limit' setting today because `++count > Infinity` at L22513 is always false) and numeric strings ('5' works today through `>` coercion); use `Number(options.maxErrorsPerMinute) > 0 ? Number(options.maxErrorsPerMinute) : 10`, which keeps both and still maps 0/undefined/NaN to 10.; L21860-21865 grouping: `existingError.record` is always null because nothing ever writes `record` (L22459, L22471). After `const record = await this._logToKnackTable(errorData)` (L21876) do `this._errorHashes.get(hash)?.record = record` so a grouped duplicate resolves the original record as the JSDoc at L21845 promises; return the hash from _processErrorGroup or compute it once in handleError to avoid hashing twice. This changes the resolved value for grouped duplicates from null to an object - inside the declared `Promise<Object\|null>`, but any caller using the result's truthiness as 'a new row was written' changes; listed in behaviourRisks.; L21960-22247 six wrappers -> one private core `#wrap(fn, {source, captureArgs = true, captureContext = true, additionalInfo = {}, measure = false, onError})` that returns the async wrapper; each public method becomes 3-8 lines that passes its own `onError`: wrapFunction/executeFunction(rethrowError) -> `(e) => { throw e }`; tryCatch -> `(e) => ({success:false, error:e, message:e.message})`; safeExecute/executeFunction(returnDefaultOnError) -> `() => defaultValue`; monitorPerformance/monitorApiCall -> rethrow after adding `responseTime`/`performanceContext`/`apiParams`. Exact per-method catch semantics (rethrow vs default vs result object, `console.error` in wrapKnackListener L22275) are preserved; only bodies shrink. The core must also reproduce three per-method differences or stored records change: (i) key precedence - executeFunction/wrapFunction/safeExecute spread `...additionalInfo` FIRST so the generated `functionArgs`/`knackContext` overwrite caller keys (L22203-22218), whereas tryCatch (L21982-21991), monitorPerformance (L22027-22037) and monitorApiCall (L22094-22099) spread `...additionalInfo` LAST so caller keys win - give each method its own `buildInfo` callback instead of one shared merge order; (ii) default-source rules - 'Anonymous Function' (L21992), 'Performance Monitor' (L22039), 'Execute Function' via the destructuring default at L22190 (which also applies when wrapFunction forwards `undefined`), and monitorApiCall's parameter default (L22051) under which an explicit '' is passed through as the source; (iii) monitorPerformance attaches NO `knackContext` today (L22027-22037), so call the core with `captureContext: false` for it, or flag that monitorPerformance errors will start populating the knackContext field.; L22051-22108 monitorApiCall: the positional guess `recordId: args[2]` is wrong for KnackAPI.createRecord(sceneId, viewId, recordData, options) (L14465) - args[2] is the payload. Derive `requestType` first via `[['create','POST'],['update','PUT'],['delete','DELETE'],['get','GET']].find(([k]) => fnName.includes(k))?.[1]`, then set `recordId` only for PUT/DELETE/GET and `requestData` to `args[2]` for POST, `args[3]` for PUT. Replace the if/else-if chain (L22081-L22091) with that lookup; drop `endTime` temporaries (`performance.now() - startTime`). The reshaped `apiParams` changes the stored additionalInfo JSON for POST calls (the payload moves from `recordId` to `requestData`), and `getRecords(sceneId, viewId, options)` still has its options object labelled `recordId` under the GET rule - acceptable, but it belongs in the stored-text behaviour risk.; L22249-22286 wrapKnackListener: both branches of `eventSelector` (L22279-22281) yield `${eventType}.${viewId}` - collapse to one template literal. The binding at L22283 is policy exception 1 and MUST go through the foundation primitive `onKnackEvent(eventName, handler)` (already defined in v2/00-foundation.js:204 - it takes the full namespaced name and returns an unbind function): `onKnackEvent(`${eventType}.${viewId}`, wrappedHandler)`; raw `$(document).on` is not permitted in V2 implementation code even for lifecycle events. Keep returning `this`. Replace `arguments` (L22263) with rest params. Document that only synchronous throws are caught (an async handler's rejection escapes to `unhandledrejection`).; L22318-22335 _setupContextTracking: bind each event through `onKnackEvent(`knack-${eventType}.any`, handler)` (policy exception 1 routed through the foundation primitive, not raw `L22318-22335 _setupContextTracking: ); move the event list to `static TRACKED_EVENTS` and keep it IDENTICAL to the six events at L22320 - do not add 'record-create'/'records-render' in wave 3: a `knack-records-render` payload is (event, view, records), so it would reset `contextRecordId` to null and change `contextEventType` (and the grouping hash at L22527) for every error after a grid render.; L22340-22357 _getCurrentSceneId: `typeof Knack !== 'undefined' && Knack.router?.current_scene_key` -> early return; DOM fallback should call the shared getBackgroundSceneId() (L18641) and `slice(3)` the 'kn-' prefix instead of `document.querySelector('.kn-scene')` (which can pick a modal scene depending on DOM order) and `.replace('kn-', '')`. `_extractSceneIdFromUrl` (L22406-22414) matches `/scene_(\d+)/` which never occurs in Knack's slug-based hash URLs; keep it as the last resort for compatibility but say so in its JSDoc. When delegating, keep `Knack.router.current_scene_key` as step one and use getBackgroundSceneId() only for the DOM step: getBackgroundSceneId returns the raw DOM id ('kn-scene_1151') and ignores modal scenes, while the router key reflects the modal scene when one is open, so they answer different questions; keep the `startsWith('kn-scene_')` guard before `slice(3)`.; L22584-22627 _captureErrorData: use `static CONTEXT_FIELDS` instead of the inline list (L22596-22600); KEEP the copy-from-additionalInfo loop (L22602) over all eight CONTEXT_FIELDS: consumers can pass `contextSceneId`, `contextViewId` etc. explicitly in additionalInfo, and today those values reach the dedicated fields whenever `captureKnackContext` is false or `separateContextFields` is false (then `_captureContextData` returns `{}` at L22707 and nothing overwrites them); dropping the loop would silently blank those fields. Only when both options are on does L22618 overwrite the six `context*` keys. Keep `errorStack: error instanceof Error ? error.stack : new Error().stack` (capture-site stack for string errors is useful).; L22775-22820 _logToKnackTable: mark `async`; `for (const [key, fieldId] of Object.entries(fieldMap))` with `continue`; build the filtered additionalInfo with `Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(errorData.additionalInfo).filter(([k]) => (k === 'additionalInfo' \|\| !Object.hasOwn(fieldMap, k)) && !KnackError.CONTEXT_FIELDS.includes(k)))` (same exemption of the literal key 'additionalInfo' as L22797); run `JSON.stringify` (L22814) through the cycle-safe serialiser so a Knack view/Backbone model or jQuery object in additionalInfo no longer throws and silently loses the record (the throw is caught at L21877 and only console.error'd). The serialiser call must keep `space: 2` (L22814 uses `JSON.stringify(x, null, 2)`) so the stored blob's layout is unchanged; the Node -> nodeName mapping will change the stored text for DOM values that previously serialised as `{}` (e.g. `{ viewElement }` at L9063) - listed in behaviourRisks.; L21789-21800 constructor: replace the eight `x !== undefined ? x : true` ternaries with `options.captureUserData ?? true` etc.; keep `maxErrorsPerMinute` as `Number.isFinite(options.maxErrorsPerMinute) && options.maxErrorsPerMinute > 0 ? options.maxErrorsPerMinute : 10` (the current `\|\| 10` maps 0 to 10 - `?? 10` would not, and 0 would rate-limit every error). Keep `this.api = new KnackAPI({debug:false, showSpinner:false})` eager (L21803): a lazy getter would turn the public `api` property into a getter-only accessor and break `instance.api = customApi`. Note `??` differs from the current `!== undefined` ternary only for an explicit `null`: today `captureUserData: null` disables the flag (null is falsy), with `??` it would enable it. Acceptable, but record it in the changelog, and keep `sceneId`/`viewId`/`fieldMap`/`ignoredErrors` on `\|\|` (L21791-21793, L21797) so '' and null still map to the defaults. Keep `ignoredErrors` BY REFERENCE (no `[...]` copy): `ignoreError()` (L22126) pushes into the caller's array and consumers may share one ignore list across instances.; L21860-21865 grouping: `existingError.record` is always null because nothing ever writes `record` (L22459, L22471). After `const record = await this._logToKnackTable(errorData)` (L21878) write it back with `const entry = this._errorHashes.get(hash); if (entry) entry.record = record;` - NOT `this._errorHashes.get(hash)?.record = record`, which is a SyntaxError (an optional chain is not a valid assignment target). Return the hash from _processErrorGroup (or compute it once in handleError) so the error is hashed once. This makes a grouped duplicate resolve the first logged record as the JSDoc at L21845 promises; it stays inside `Promise<Object\|null>` but is a behaviour change for callers that test the resolved value for 'newly logged' - listed in behaviourRisks.; L21894-21925 wrapEventHandler: keep `function` (the wrapper must forward the caller's dynamic `this` to the handler) but replace `arguments` (L21912) with rest params `...args` and `handler.apply(this, args)`; `eventType.replace('knack-', '')` -> `eventType.replace(/^knack-/, '')`; the `const context = {...}` literal can be assigned straight to `self._lastKnownContext`. Fix the payload mapping for scene events: `knack-scene-render` delivers `(event, scene)` so `view.key` at L21901 is the SCENE key ('scene_12') and is stored as `viewId`; branch on `eventType === 'knack-scene-render'` -> `{ sceneId: view?.key ?? self._getCurrentSceneId(), viewId: null }`. Stored-text change listed in behaviourRisks.; L21928-21953 setupGlobalErrorHandling: use `this.boundHandleError`-style arrow listeners as now; add an idempotence guard (`if (this._globalHandlersBound) return this;`) so a second call does not double-log every window error - flagged in behaviourRisks as a (benign) behaviour change; keep the `event.error \|\| event.message` and `event.reason instanceof Error` coercions exactly.; L21960-22247 six wrappers -> one shared core `_wrap(fn, {source, captureArgs = true, captureContext = true, additionalInfo = {}, measure = false, extraInfo, onError})` that returns the async wrapper; each public method becomes 3-8 lines that passes its own `onError`: wrapFunction/executeFunction(rethrowError) -> `(e) => { throw e }`; tryCatch -> `(e) => ({success:false, error:e, message:e.message})`; safeExecute/executeFunction(returnDefaultOnError) -> `() => defaultValue`; monitorPerformance/monitorApiCall -> rethrow after adding `responseTime`/`performanceContext`/`apiParams`. Name it `_wrap` (underscore), not `#wrap`: the file has zero `#private` members, this class's own convention is `_underscore`, and a `#` core is unreachable to a consumer subclass that overrides one public wrapper. The collapse must preserve, per method, everything that reaches the stored record and the grouping hash: (1) the default source strings - executeFunction/wrapFunction/safeExecute `fn.name \|\| 'Execute Function'` (L22190), tryCatch `source \|\| fn.name \|\| 'Anonymous Function'` (L21996), monitorPerformance `source \|\| fn.name \|\| 'Performance Monitor'` (L22038), monitorApiCall default parameter `'Knack API Call'` (L22051, so `null`/'' pass through); (2) the capture set - tryCatch: functionArgs + knackContext; monitorPerformance: functionArgs + responseTime + performanceContext and NO knackContext; monitorApiCall: apiParams + responseTime + knackContext and NO functionArgs; executeFunction: governed by captureArgs/captureContext; (3) the spread precedence - executeFunction spreads `...additionalInfo` first and lets the computed functionArgs/knackContext override it (L22205-22216), the other three spread `...additionalInfo` LAST so the caller's keys win (L21990, L22037, L22100). Exact per-method catch semantics (rethrow vs default vs result object, `console.error` at L22272 in wrapKnackListener) are preserved; only bodies shrink.; L21983-21988, L22028-22030, L22208-22213: one `_serialiseArgs(args)` (underscore, matching the class convention; or the file-level `safeStringify`) with a single replacer (function -> 'function() { ... }', Node -> nodeName, Window -> 'Window', plus a WeakSet cycle guard so a Backbone view or jQuery object in the arguments cannot throw 'Converting circular structure to JSON' inside the catch block). monitorPerformance's '[Function]' spelling (L22029) is unified to the common one - see behaviourRisks.; L22051-22108 monitorApiCall: the positional guess `recordId: args[2]` (L22074) is wrong for `createRecord(sceneId, viewId, recordData, options)` (L14465) where args[2] is the payload, and also for `getRecords(sceneId, viewId, options)` (L14233) where args[2] is an options object - so 'recordId only for GET' would still mislabel getRecords. Derive `requestType` first via `[['create','POST'],['update','PUT'],['delete','DELETE'],['get','GET']].find(([k]) => fnName.includes(k))?.[1]` (same precedence as the chain at L22083-L22091), then set `recordId` only when `typeof args[2] === 'string'` (updateRecord L14848, deleteRecord L15059, getRecord L15203) and `requestData` to the first plain-object argument after the ids (args[2] for POST/getRecords options, args[3] for PUT). Drop the `endTime` temporaries (`performance.now() - startTime`). The `apiParams` text stored in additionalInfo changes for POST/getRecords calls - listed in behaviourRisks.; L22138-22151 wrapAllMethods: keep `for...in` - but for the right reason: methods declared with `class` syntax are non-enumerable, so NEITHER `for...in` nor `Object.entries` sees them on a class instance; the two differ only for enumerable INHERITED properties (constructor-function prototypes assigned with `Ctor.prototype.m = fn`, `Object.create(proto)` objects), which `for...in` wraps today and `Object.entries` would skip - a behaviour change. Write the body as a single ternary assignment; do not add an `Object.hasOwn` filter. Document in JSDoc that the result is a plain copy: wrapped methods run with `this` = the copy, so they see the shallow-copied own properties as of wrap time, not the original object.; L22318-22335 _setupContextTracking: keep the jQuery binding (`knack-${eventType}.any`, L22323) for the same reason as wrapKnackListener (route it through the `onKnackEvent` primitive); move the event list to `static TRACKED_EVENTS`. Fix the payload mapping, which today assumes `(event, view, record)` for every event: `knack-scene-render` delivers `(event, scene)`, so L22325 stores the SCENE key ('scene_12') in `_lastKnownContext.viewId`, and because getCurrentContext() only falls back to the DOM when `viewId` is falsy (L22307), every error raised after a scene render and before the next view event is stored with `contextViewId: 'scene_12'`; branch: `eventType === 'scene-render' ? { sceneId: view?.key ?? this._getCurrentSceneId(), viewId: null } : { sceneId: this._getCurrentSceneId(), viewId: view?.key ?? null }`. `knack-record-delete` delivers the record id as the third argument per the policy's event table (unverified against live docs), so `record?.id` (L22328) yields null exactly for deletes: use `typeof record === 'string' ? record : record?.id ?? null`. Optionally add 'record-create' and 'records-render' (documented Knack events that are currently untracked; records-render's third argument is an array, so recordId stays null) - an additive context improvement, not required. Stored-text change listed in behaviourRisks.; L22584-22627 _captureErrorData: use `static CONTEXT_FIELDS` instead of the inline list (L22596-22600) but KEEP the copy-from-additionalInfo loop (L22602-22604) as `for (const k of KnackError.CONTEXT_FIELDS) if (additionalInfo[k]) errorData[k] = additionalInfo[k];`. It is not dead: _captureContextData only overwrites the six `context*` keys when BOTH captureKnackContext and separateContextFields are on (L22707); with `separateContextFields: false` it returns `{}`, with `captureKnackContext: false` it is not called (L22617), and in its catch path (L22729) nothing is overwritten - in all three cases a caller-supplied `contextSceneId`/`contextViewId` etc. only reaches the dedicated field through this loop. Keep `errorStack: error instanceof Error ? error.stack : new Error().stack` (capture-site stack for string errors is useful). Coerce `errorSource: String(errorSource ?? 'Unknown')` here so `_updatePerformanceMetrics` cannot throw on a non-string source.; L22632-22652 _captureUserData: drop the redundant `this.options.captureUserData` re-check (the caller already tests it at L22607); `Knack && Knack.getUserAttributes` (L22636) sits in the `if` condition OUTSIDE the try block, so when `Knack` is undeclared (e.g. a node:test harness) the ReferenceError is NOT caught - it escapes _captureErrorData and rejects handleError, which the fire-and-forget wrapper callers never catch. Use `const attrs = globalThis.Knack?.getUserAttributes?.(); if (!attrs) return {};` and `globalThis.Knack?.getUserRoleNames?.()`, consistent with the `typeof Knack` guard at L22342.; L22775-22820 _logToKnackTable: leave it a plain method returning the KnackAPI promise (there is no await in the body; adding `async` would only convert a synchronous TypeError into a rejection, a thrown-errors change under the policy with no benefit); `for (const [key, fieldId] of Object.entries(fieldMap))` with `continue`; build the filtered additionalInfo with `Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(errorData.additionalInfo).filter(([k]) => (k === 'additionalInfo' \|\| !Object.hasOwn(fieldMap, k)) && !KnackError.CONTEXT_FIELDS.includes(k)))` (same exemption of the literal key 'additionalInfo' as L22797); run `JSON.stringify` (L22814) through the cycle-safe serialiser so a Knack view/Backbone model or jQuery object in additionalInfo no longer throws and silently loses the record (the throw is caught at L21877 and only console.error'd).; L22828-22881 _getBrowserInfo: table-drive it - `{ name, test: /Edg/i, version: /Edge?\/(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)/i }` per browser and `const hit = TABLE.find((b) => b.test.test(ua)); return hit ? { name: hit.name, version: ua.match(hit.version)?.[1] ?? 'Unknown' } : { name: 'Unknown', version: 'Unknown' };` - removes the 30-line switch. Ordering bug: 'Opera' (L22834) sits after 'Chrome' and 'Safari' but Opera's UA contains both, so Opera is reported as Chrome; order the table Edge, Opera, Chrome, Firefox, Safari, IE; Opera's version regex must keep both spellings, `/(?:OPR\|Opera)\/(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)/i`, to match the dual `match` at L22859. Detection-output change flagged in behaviourRisks.; L22900-22916 _getOperatingSystem: `return SYSTEMS.find((s) => s.pattern.test(userAgent))?.name ?? 'Unknown OS';` with the table as a frozen static kept in the CURRENT order (Windows, Windows Phone, macOS, iOS, Android, Linux - L22901-22908). The only overlapping pair is Android before Linux (Android UAs contain 'Linux'); macOS is listed before iOS today and that is harmless because iPhone/iPad UAs say 'like Mac OS X', not 'Macintosh'. Do not reorder. Risks: jQuery is load-bearing here, not incidental: `$(document).on(`${eventType}.${viewId}`)` (L22283) and `$(document).on(`knack-${eventType}.any`)` (L22323) receive events that Knack raises through jQuery `.trigger`; a 'modernised' `document.addEventListener('knack-view-render.view_12', ...)` would never fire and context tracking plus wrapKnackListener would silently stop working. Keep `$` for these two sites (or route them through a single foundation helper that wraps `$(document).on`).; All wrappers (L21894, L21975, L22010, L22051, L22189) turn synchronous functions into async ones: the wrapped function's return becomes a Promise, side effects still run synchronously up to the first await, and `return false` from a wrapped jQuery event handler no longer calls preventDefault/stopPropagation. This is existing behaviour that callers may depend on in either direction; preserve it exactly, do not 'fix' by returning sync results for sync fns.; wrapKnackListener's inner handler (L22254-22277) is synchronous and only catches synchronous throws; an async handler's rejection bypasses it and surfaces as `unhandledrejection` (then via setupGlobalErrorHandling if enabled). Making it `async` would also change the handler's return value to a Promise - keep as is and document.; safeExecute (L22160) resolves a Promise (via executeFunction immediate:true, L22234) even though its JSDoc says it returns the value; existing callers may `await` it. Making it synchronous would be a signature change requiring a shim - leave it asynchronous.; Fire-and-forget `self.handleError(...)` calls inside catch blocks (L21986, L22030, L22097, L22220, L22272) are not awaited; any synchronous throw inside handleError's capture phase (e.g. `errorData.errorSource.includes` on a non-string source at L22547, or JSON.stringify on a circular `additionalInfo` at L22814 is already inside try) becomes an unhandled rejection, which setupGlobalErrorHandling(true) feeds back into handleError (bounded only by maxErrorsPerMinute). V2 should wrap the capture phase in try/catch with a console fallback - a behaviour change in the error path only.; Stored field text will change where the port fixes defects: `contextViewId` (and thus the grouping hash, L22527) changes from 'view_12-field_3' to 'view_12' when an input is focused; `browserName` 'Chrome' -> 'Opera' for Opera users; `knackContextFormatted` time text changes from browser-locale `toLocaleString()` to fixed en-GB; `functionArgs` from monitorPerformance uses 'function() { ... }' instead of '[Function]'; `pageLoadTime`/`domReadyTime` shift by a few ms when moving off the deprecated `performance.timing`. None affect code paths, but any Knack report/filter built on the literal strings will see the new values.; Grouping and rate-limit state (`_errorHashes`, `_errorCount`) is per instance and `_errorHashes` is never evicted (L22468-22474); over a long-lived SPA session it grows by one entry per distinct (name\|message\|source\|scene\|view\|event) tuple. Adding eviction (e.g. drop entries older than an hour on each insert) is a safe memory fix but changes when a repeat is treated as fresh if done carelessly - keep the one-hour reset semantics at L22452-22462.; setupGlobalErrorHandling (L21928) is not idempotent: each call adds another `error`/`unhandledrejection` listener, so calling it from two scripts double-logs. Adding a guard is a small behaviour change (second call becomes a no-op) - acceptable but must be called out in the changelog.; The constructor eagerly instantiates KnackAPI (L21803) with `{debug:false, showSpinner:false}`; the KnackAPI port must keep those option names and defaults, and `instance.api` must stay a plain writable property (consumers may inject a stub API in tests, and `instance.api.getRecords(...)` is reachable public API).; Dropping the `Object.assign(additionalInfo, ...)` mutation at L21855 means a caller's object is no longer enriched with `contextSceneId`/`contextViewId`/... after `await handleError(err, obj)`. No caller in this file reads back from the object it passed; the stored record is unchanged. Negligible but technically a side-effect change.; _validateOptions (L22420-22431) only console.errors; it never throws, so a misconfigured instance silently degrades to console-only logging. Keep that (throwing from the constructor would be a thrown-errors signature change).; `Knack.router.current_scene_key` (L22342) and `Knack.getUserRoleNames()` (L22642) are relied on from memory - unverified against live docs. Keep the DOM fallbacks that exist today.; jQuery is load-bearing here, not incidental: `$(document).on(`${eventType}.${viewId}`)` (L22283) and `$(document).on(`knack-${eventType}.any`)` (L22323) receive events that Knack raises through jQuery `.trigger`; a 'modernised' `document.addEventListener('knack-view-render.view_12', ...)` would never fire and context tracking plus wrapKnackListener would silently stop working. Both sites are policy exception 1 and must be bound through the foundation `onKnackEvent(eventName, handler)` primitive (v2/00-foundation.js:204), not raw `jQuery is load-bearing here, not incidental: `$(document).on(`${eventType}.${viewId}`)` (L22283) and `$(document).on(`knack-${eventType}.any`)` (L22323) receive events that Knack raises through jQuery `.trigger`; a 'modernised' `document.addEventListener('knack-view-render.view_12', ...)` would never fire and context tracking plus wrapKnackListener would silently stop working. ; behaviour is identical because onKnackEvent is `$(document).on` underneath, and the unbind function it returns can be kept internally for a future dispose without changing the public surface.; Stored field text will change where the port fixes defects: `contextViewId` (and thus the grouping hash, L22527) changes from 'view_12-field_3' to 'view_12' when an input is focused; `browserName` 'Chrome' -> 'Opera' for Opera users; `knackContextFormatted` time text changes from browser-locale `toLocaleString()` to fixed en-GB; `functionArgs` from monitorPerformance uses 'function() { ... }' instead of '[Function]'; `pageLoadTime`/`domReadyTime` shift by a few ms when moving off the deprecated `performance.timing`. None affect code paths, but any Knack report/filter built on the literal strings will see the new values. Also: `apiParams` from monitorApiCall is reshaped for POST calls (payload moves from `recordId` to `requestData`); DOM/jQuery values inside additionalInfo serialise as nodeName strings instead of `{}` once the cycle-safe serialiser replaces `JSON.stringify` at L22814; monitorPerformance errors gain a `knackContext` entry if the shared core captures context by default (none today at L22027-22037).; handleError's resolved value for grouped duplicates changes from always-null (L21864; `record` is never written at L22459/L22471) to the first logged record. The declared `Promise<Object\|null>` is unchanged, but a caller that reads the result's truthiness as 'this call wrote a row' will now see true for repeats.; Collapsing the six wrappers onto one core risks changing which value wins when a caller passes its own `functionArgs` or `knackContext` in additionalInfo: executeFunction/wrapFunction/safeExecute let the generated keys win (spread first, L22203-22218); tryCatch/monitorPerformance/monitorApiCall let the caller win (spread last, L21991/L22037/L22099). Each method's merge order and default-source string must be reproduced exactly.; Option coercion: `?? true` treats an explicit `null` as enabled where the current `!== undefined` ternaries (L21792-21800) treat it as disabled; `Number.isFinite(...)` rejects `Infinity` and numeric strings for maxErrorsPerMinute, both of which work today (L21796, L22513). Keep the current acceptance set.; `this.options.ignoredErrors` keeps the caller's array by reference (L21797) and `ignoreError()` pushes into it (L22129), so a consumer's array is mutated today; copying it (`[...options.ignoredErrors]`) would be the opposite behaviour change - keep the reference.; `#private` methods/fields (`#wrap`, `#serialiseArgs`, `#globalHandlersBound`) throw TypeError when a public method runs with a `this` that is not a KnackError instance (e.g. `const w = errorHandler.wrapFunction; w(fn)`, or the handler's own methods passed through `wrapAllMethods`); such calls fail differently or work today. Tooling is not a blocker: eslint/generate-globals.mjs parses with `ecmaVersion: 'latest'` (L78) and terser 5.34 handles ES2022.; Order of the two gates in handleError matters: `_shouldIgnoreError` runs before `_isRateLimited` (L21850) and `_isRateLimited` increments `_errorCount` (L22513) even for errors later grouped or not logged; swapping the order or short-circuiting differently would make ignored errors consume the per-minute budget and change `getPerformanceMetrics().errorRate.perMinute`.; Fire-and-forget `self.handleError(...)` calls inside catch blocks (L21986, L22030, L22097, L22220, L22272) are not awaited; any synchronous throw inside handleError's capture phase becomes an unhandled rejection, which setupGlobalErrorHandling(true) feeds back into handleError (bounded only by maxErrorsPerMinute). Concrete throw paths today: `errorData.errorSource.includes` on a non-string source (L22547), and `Knack && Knack.getUserAttributes` at L22636, which is in the `if` condition outside that method's try block, so an undeclared `Knack` (node:test harness) rejects every handleError call. JSON.stringify on a circular `additionalInfo` (L22814) is already inside handleError's try at L21877 and is only console.error'd. V2 should wrap the capture phase in try/catch with a console fallback - a behaviour change in the error path only.; Stored field text will change where the port fixes defects: `contextViewId` changes from 'view_12-field_3' to 'view_12' when an input is focused, and from 'scene_12' (the scene key leaking in via knack-scene-render) to the real view key or the DOM fallback; `browserName` 'Chrome' -> 'Opera' for Opera users; `knackContextFormatted` time text changes from browser-locale `toLocaleString()` to fixed en-GB; `functionArgs` from monitorPerformance uses 'function() { ... }' instead of '[Function]'; `apiParams.recordId`/`requestData` from monitorApiCall are relabelled for createRecord/getRecords; `pageLoadTime`/`domReadyTime` shift by a few ms when moving off the deprecated `performance.timing`; `contextRecordId` is populated for record-delete events. The grouping hash (L22527) changes with contextViewId, but `_errorHashes` is in-memory per page load, so this has no cross-deploy effect. None affect code paths, but any Knack report/filter built on the literal strings will see the new values.; Knack event payload shapes: `knack-scene-render` delivers `(event, scene)`, not `(event, view, record)`, so both context trackers (L21901 in wrapEventHandler and L22325 in _setupContextTracking) store the scene key as `viewId`; since getCurrentContext() only consults the DOM when `viewId` is falsy (L22307), errors raised between a scene render and the next view event are stored under contextViewId='scene_x' (whether this window is long or short depends on Knack's scene/view render ordering - unverified against live docs). Fixing it is a stored-text change; not fixing it keeps a known-wrong value. Recommend fixing, with the changelog note.; Grouped duplicates currently always resolve `null` from handleError (L21864, `record` never written). Once the port stores the first record, a duplicate resolves that record object; this stays within `Promise<Object\|null>` but any caller using a truthy result to mean 'a new row was created' (e.g. to count logged errors) changes behaviour. Document in the handleError JSDoc.; `options.ignoredErrors` is held by reference (L21797) and `ignoreError()` pushes into it (L22126). A port that defensively copies the array (`[...options.ignoredErrors]`) would silently stop apps that share one ignore list across instances, or that push onto their own array after construction, from seeing the additions. Keep the reference.; Collapsing the six wrappers onto one core must not homogenise what each one stores: the per-method default source strings ('Execute Function', 'Anonymous Function', 'Performance Monitor', 'Knack API Call'), the per-method capture set (monitorPerformance has no knackContext, monitorApiCall has no functionArgs) and the spread precedence (executeFunction lets computed keys override the caller's additionalInfo at L22205-22216; tryCatch/monitorPerformance/monitorApiCall let the caller's keys win at L21990/L22037/L22100) all land in errorSource (part of the grouping hash) and the stored additionalInfo blob. A tidy unified core that changes any of them changes stored rows for existing wrapped functions.; If wrapKnackListener (L22283) and _setupContextTracking (L22323) move onto the `onKnackEvent` foundation primitive, that primitive must invoke the handler exactly as jQuery does - `this` = document and the full trigger argument list - because wrapKnackListener forwards both (`handler.apply(this, arguments)`, L22263) and consumer handlers may rely on `this`. An arrow-based or addEventListener-style primitive would change the handler's `this` and silently drop extra trigger arguments. |
